LUCKYDEX — MASTER PROJECT RULES AND DEVELOPMENT CONTRACT
=========================================================
Document version: 2.53
Compiled: 21 November 2026
Current inspected source: v0.69.364 Ancient Origins Stage 2, based on verified v0.69.363 Stage 1 (GitHub run #652 success); v0.69.364 passes local regression but awaits GitHub APK verification. Source-relative ZIP extraction into ~/lucky-dex/source/.
Recommended repository location: ~/lucky-dex/source/docs/LUCKYDEX_MASTER_RULES.txt
Project: LuckyDex — Android application/PWA, managed with Termux and GitHub

PURPOSE
-------
This file is the long-term source of truth for developing LuckyDex across new ChatGPT conversations and many future Pokémon card expansions. Read it BEFORE implementing features, fixing bugs, changing a game rule, choosing card metadata, or packaging a patch.

This is a living contract, not a claim that every rule is already correctly implemented. It records (A) user-approved design requirements, (B) behaviour observed in the inspected source, (C) known exceptions/bugs, and (D) proposals needing approval. If these conflict, DO NOT quietly assume the existing code wins. Flag the difference, verify the previous decision and printed card text, and ask the user only when a new design choice really is required.

Use these labels consistently:
  [LOCKED]    Explicitly agreed or repeatedly reinforced. Do not change without permission.
  [CURRENT]   Observed in the inspected code; not automatically an approved design decision.
  [EXCEPTION] Documented LuckyDex conversion from official rules; preserve its scope.
  [CHECK]     Historical disagreement, unverified interpretation or unresolved issue.
  [PROPOSED] New recommendation; not agreed until user accepts it.
  [BUG]       Behaviour known to be wrong; do not memorialise as a rule.

RULE PRECEDENCE AND CONFLICT MANAGEMENT
---------------------------------------
1. User's latest explicit instruction or approval has highest design authority.
2. An explicitly documented, previously approved LuckyDex adaptation takes priority over a generic assumption about official TCG mechanics.
3. For individual cards, authoritative printed text and official errata determine the card's own effect unless a specific LuckyDex conversion is approved.
4. This file defines shared implementation, UI and testing expectations. The code is the implementation to audit, not the final authority on correctness.
5. A test confirms intended behaviour only if its expectation agrees with the rules. A passing test based on a wrong assumption is still a bug.
6. Never infer that an undocumented feature is authorised merely because a similar card or older set did something that way.
7. When two instructions conflict: preserve existing user experience, document the contradiction as [CHECK], avoid unrelated changes, and seek an explicit decision before altering the game rule.
8. Date or version every revised rule. Keep a change history; never silently rewrite a historical decision or disguise a bug as a design adaptation.

=======================================================================
10.14L [LOCKED] C-RELEASE-GATE-019 — DIAMOND & PEARL LV.X AND PRINT COMPLETION (v2.21)
  • Every registered 130-print dp1 card has a battle effect, real metadata and playable source registration; no placeholder/no-op claim is allowed for a card that has a printed attack or Power.
  • LV.X cards may LEVEL UP only the matching species' Active previous-level Pokémon from hand, not a Benched Pokémon and not a card entered/evolved that turn. Level-up is NOT an Evolution event and never retriggers 'when evolved from hand' Powers. LV.X preserves all prior levels in stack, attached Energy, Tools and damage; it inherits attack and Power/Body options of the immediately previous printed level (not an unrelated earlier evolution).
  • All LV.X drawn or opponent-built decks must contain the entire previous-level family, including Basic, Stage 1 and Stage 2. Rank Level-Up after Stage 2 in deckbuilding and keep each prerequisite owned and legal.
  • Empoleon LV.X Supreme Command temporarily holds up to two concealed opponent-hand cards, without revealing identities, until the END of the opponent's next turn. Cards are not in hand or in play while held; restore actual card references at expiry. Hydro Impact may target opponent Bench and imposes the next-attack lock.
  • Infernape LV.X Flare Up requires 8 real Fire Energy in the discard ledger/physical pile or does nothing. On success return exactly 8 to deck, shuffle and resolve its printed damage. Burning Head chooses one card from top three and discards the others.
  • Torterra LV.X Forest Murmurs requires the owner have MORE Prizes remaining than opponent and a legal Bench target. Vigorous Dash has targeted opponent Bench damage and real self-damage.
  • Dusknoir Dark Palm requires FOUR opposing Benched Pokémon (not a hard-coded three-slot conversion), and shuffles complete evolution/attached-card stack to the owner's deck. Luxray Gleam Eyes and Rhyperior Earth Fissure trigger on evolution FROM HAND only; both must offer meaningful decline choices. Electivire Intense Voltage needs Elekid underneath, supports repeating within the same turn, and offers only truly movable Lightning Energy source cards. Shiftry Darkness Fan needs Active Shiftry and a selectable Evolved opponent Benched target.
  • A printed same-name Special Energy effect must remain distinct from Basic Energy Zone counters. Track real destinations for all moved/discarded cards; no silent auto-choices when the card says 'may' or 'choose'.
  • A source-level 130/130 catalogue ledger is NOT by itself proof of Android correctness. Keep active device, UX, long CPU matches and full cross-set regressions as separate evidence categories. Never claim a build/install or device test was performed unless it actually happened.
=======================================================================

10.14M [LOCKED] C-UI-CHOICE-020 / C-UI-PRIZE-021 — PRINTED ATTACK SELECTION AND RESOLUTION BARRIERS (v2.22)
  • Any action producing multiple legal choices MUST display a user-meaningful difference from the underlying rule action, not just the repeated attacker art/name or the nominal base damage. Copy-attack selectors (e.g. Diamond & Pearl Clefable Metronome) show the COPIED Pokémon's print art, copied attack name, base damage / effect-only classification, and its actual printed effect. A printed 0-damage effect must not be marketed as an identical "0 DMG" option.
  • Attack selection labels must use choiceCardId+choiceAttackIndex to resolve the printed target move. Never select/preview a random outcome to manufacture damage text or reveal hidden cards; describe printed effects, not hypothetical outcomes. The attack resolves through the original action unchanged; menu rendering must never modify attack semantics.
  • Effect-choice attack options must visibly show the attack name alongside card art. Display distinct action.choiceLabel descriptions for any option with matching artwork, including Trainers, coin contingencies and voluntary discard choices. Check that each set's multi-option attacks have distinct visible labels in generated legal-action states.
  • In Classic TCG, a human Prize-pick modal MUST NOT appear until the originating action's visible coin flips, attack animation, secondary damage/status and knockouts have finished. The battle core may record a pending Prize choice immediately, but the renderer holds its interactive choice while the presentation plays, including when a player effect-choice itself causes a KO.
  • The hold releases upon natural animation completion or the player's explicit Skip, and must fail open when animations/assets are unavailable. Do not lock the player in the Prize screen; do not skip the official human Prize position choice; preserve face-up Prize states, multiple consecutive Prize claims and CPU selections. Reset hold when abandoning/restarting battle.
  • Regression: assert distinct copied attack choices for 2+ moves; assert correct copied card art; assert no Prize modal during a pending attack/coin/KO sequence; assert choice returns after presentation, including no-animation fallback; assert non-Prize choices remain available; run DP1 attack-choice label sweep. Test real device animation ordering separately.

PART 1 — PERMANENT DEVELOPMENT / HANDOFF RULES
=======================================================================

1.1 Source of truth and delivery
  [LOCKED] The working source directory is ~/lucky-dex/source/ . Downloaded ZIPs are handoff artefacts, not the canonical editable project.
  [LOCKED] Continue from the latest supplied/current source. Never overwrite recent features by rebuilding from an old ZIP or by recreating parts of the app unnecessarily.
  [LOCKED] Do NOT use Codex unless the user specifically requests Codex.
  [LOCKED] Once an agreed feature/fix is actionable, implement it and provide a testable patch instead of only describing intended work.
  [LOCKED] Prefer changed-files-only ZIP patches, with a small, clearly stated extraction target. NEVER mix a repository-root ZIP with a source-relative ZIP without explicitly identifying which it is.
  [LOCKED] Do not overwrite the user's collection, saves, deck libraries, card art, release signing material, app ID or unrelated minigames.
  [LOCKED] Don't push to the user's GitHub or claim deployment has occurred without actually doing it and being authorised.
  [LOCKED] Provide a working download link to the created patch and accurate Termux unzip instructions. Avoid release instructions that guess the installation path.
  [LOCKED] Keep Android versionName, versionCode, JS build labels, on-screen version displays and tests consistent. Every shipped change should have an identifiable version.
  [LOCKED] Preserve the permanent signing identity and upgrade compatibility for the existing installed app.
  [LOCKED] Do not claim actual Android device testing unless it really happened. Distinguish Node/unit tests, browser UI tests, simulations and hands-on device QA.
  [LOCKED] Never pretend a file was created, a script ran, a test passed or a bug was fixed without direct evidence.

1.2 Working on an existing system
  [LOCKED] Inspect the current code and existing tests before making architectural changes. Reuse common engine paths where appropriate, but keep Classic and Pocket rule differences explicit.
  [LOCKED] Prefer fixes in shared logic when many cards are affected; add targeted per-card handling when printed effects demand it. Avoid ad-hoc patches that make only the reported screenshot work.
  [LOCKED] Audit related systems when a foundational mechanism changes: card effects, deck building, AI, Test Lab, save migration, UI and old expansions.
  [LOCKED] Each minigame includes achievements and persistent statistics. New rare events get developer-controlled test toggles so they can be exercised without grinding.
  [LOCKED] Gameplay should reward player execution/decision-making, not be a purely passive random result. Preserve each minigame's authentic Pokémon feel.
  [LOCKED] The CPU must interact with the player and game systems correctly; don't substitute fake turns or random actions merely to simulate activity.
  [LOCKED] Bugs that stop progress, crash, freeze, softlock or remove legal player choices take priority over new content.
  [LOCKED] Keep existing functionality (especially saves, ownership, collection, unlocks, rewards, progression, themes, artwork, card filters and Test Lab) unless specifically replacing it.

1.3 Rules maintenance is REQUIRED, not optional
  [LOCKED] The rules document is a deliverable for every future rule-changing patch and every new expansion. See Part 10 for the exact update protocol.
  [LOCKED] An agreed rule without an implementation test is incomplete documentation. New rules must have regression tests or an explicitly recorded, justified testing gap.
  [LOCKED] If a user reports surprising gameplay, compare the observed behaviour with this file AND the printed card before saying 'working as intended'.

=======================================================================
PART 2 — GAME-WIDE RULES AND FEATURES
=======================================================================

2.1 Identity and scope
  [LOCKED] LuckyDex is one integrated Pokémon-themed app with collection, packs, trades, Adventure, Fishing, Arcade/minigames, TCG Pocket battles and Classic TCG battles, plus developer tools.
  [LOCKED] Preserve recognizable Pokémon mechanics, card names, types, moves, conditions, art/rarity identities and region context. Custom sets and homebrew rules must be labelled as such.
  [LOCKED] Do not change existing game economies casually. Fishing has a separate currency from general Lucky Coins; card battles should use deliberately balanced rewards, not an uncontrolled farm.
  [LOCKED] Developer testing, forced rare events, test decks and sandbox matches must not grant ordinary rewards, card ownership or achievements unless a specific testing mechanism is explicitly designed to do so.
  [LOCKED] Normal purchases, unlocks, achievements and stats should persist across updates. Save compatibility is a release requirement.

2.2 Usability / mobile-first
  [LOCKED] Android portrait layout is the primary touch target. Avoid PC-only UI patterns, excessive scrolling, tiny hit targets and off-screen controls.
  [LOCKED] Avoid overlays that cover cards, text, active Energy, the Bench or primary actions. Reduce dead space; enlarge relevant information rather than decorating it.
  [LOCKED] Card art must not be stretched or cropped so that cards become unreadable. Portrait cards and landscape Stadiums retain correct proportions.
  [LOCKED] The result of an action must be visible: coin flips, energy attachments/discards, damage, status, CPU actions, rewards, errors and why an action is illegal.
  [LOCKED] Never display the same ambiguous choice repeatedly when the options have different consequences. Explain the difference at the point of choice.
  [LOCKED] Prefer touching/dragging actual cards and board slots over text-only menus; still allow a clear accessible fallback for non-board choices.
  [LOCKED] Do not leave meaningless technical banners or placeholder descriptions on the player-facing battle screen.

2.3 Current subsystems to protect
  Adventure: area progression, Kanto/other region journeys, area art, encounters and rewards; prevent softlocks after League completion. Future expansion should add area-specific interactions rather than only more screens.
  Fishing: rod/bait/shop progression, timed catch mechanic, per-location pools/silhouettes, junk/treasure/rare prizes, and separate currency. Difficulty should depend on rod and execution. Rare drops should have developer test toggles. Keep reward balance (particularly low-value Stardust) under review.
  Arcade: Eager Eevee, Barrier Ball, Pikachu Beach, Streaming Stampede, Tile/Unown puzzles and other established modes. Keep readability/accessibility aids such as Unown magnification where designed. Track scores, milestones and achievements.
  Pack opening: God Packs, rarity reveal and artwork fallback must coexist with collection data. Don't award the wrong rarity or lose the card image/identity.
  Trades: keep the complete offering visible; explain duplicate-species quantities honestly rather than inflating the number of unique offerings.
  Developer settings: allow controlled inspection of rare mechanics without mutating genuine progress.

  These are standing protection requirements, not a claim that every prior backlog item is finished.

=======================================================================
PART 3 — CLASSIC POKÉMON TCG FORMAT
=======================================================================

3.1 Core format and rules authority
  [LOCKED] Classic TCG is a CONVERTED format, not a copy of Pocket and not unmodified tabletop TCG. Use original printed card effects while obeying agreed LuckyDex conversion rules.
  [LOCKED] Standard Classic battle decks contain 30 cards at construction. Basic Energy supplied through the Energy Zone is not included in those starting 30 cards.
  [LOCKED] Default copy limit is two copies of the same printed name per deck (unless a stricter printed/card-specific restriction applies). Count same-name print variants together where required.
  [LOCKED] Evolution requirements and Basic/Stage 1/Stage 2 legality remain real, including compatible prior stages, evolution timing and relevant exceptions (such as Rare Candy where available).
  [LOCKED] Normal decks use actual owned cards. Test Lab is an explicit exception, not a way to grant real ownership.
  [LOCKED] Respect printed HP, attack damage, costs, weaknesses/resistances, retreat, Powers, Bodies, Trainer effects, Special Energy and Pokémon identity. Don't rebalance a card numerically without approval.
  [LOCKED] Preserve legitimate prize exceptions: Pokémon-ex and other cards may yield multiple prizes when their rules say so; effects like Shedinja Empty Shell must override the normal award.
  [LOCKED] Classic uses genuine opening/mulligan behaviour and legal opening Basics. Prevent illegal starts, hidden replacements and false deck exhaustion.
  [CURRENT] In v0.69.185 the core config uses 30-card decks, 7 opening cards, 3 Bench slots, 5 physical Prize cards, and a five-Prize win condition.
  [CHECK] An earlier user-approved design specified FIVE Bench spaces and FOUR points to win with different 1/2/3 prize values. THIS CONFLICTS WITH CURRENT CODE AND SCREENSHOTS (3 Bench, 5 prizes). Do not treat the current numbers as permanently approved. Do not silently change them either. Resolve explicitly when revisiting overall Classic format rules, then update this file and tests.
  [EXCEPTION] Classic EX-era references to Double Battle / two Defending Pokémon require a documented single-Active adaptation where LuckyDex cannot model two opposing Actives. Do not globally reinterpret multi-target rules; document each converted card.

3.2 Energy Zone — the most important custom rules
  [LOCKED] Basic Energy normally comes from the SEPARATE Energy Zone / ordered Energy supply, not from the 30-card starting deck.
  [LOCKED] An Energy Zone Basic Energy attachment is independent from the standard once-per-turn attachment of an Energy card from the HAND. The two allowances must not be conflated. Card effects can attach additional Energy as printed.
  [LOCKED] If the deck generates more than one chosen type, Energy generation is random but the queue order is real. The next Energy shown must be the next taken: WHAT YOU SEE IS WHAT YOU GET. No silent fishing for a preferred type or reshuffling the next preview behind the player's back.
  [LOCKED] The normal supply of Energy is effectively unlimited; the visible queue controls the in-battle order, not finite deck ownership. For card text referring to a deck, discard or hand, use the appropriate explicit destination as below.
  [LOCKED] Darkness and Metal require correct distinction: Basic types may come from the Energy Zone when enabled; old Neo/EX-era Special Darkness and Special Metal are real physical Special Energy cards with their printed special effects.
  [LOCKED] Special Energy is part of the physical 30-card deck and obeys printed attachment, effect, discard, deck-search and return rules.
  [LOCKED] Energy attached to Pokémon is represented as actual attached units, with types, source, restrictions and, for Special Energy, card identity/effect information.
  [LOCKED] A generated Basic Energy that an effect puts into the HAND is a temporary, real hand card for relevant hand-based costs, reveals, discards and hand size. Attaching it from hand obeys the normal hand-attachment allowance unless a printed effect grants an extra attachment.
  [LOCKED] An effect that explicitly searches a player's Energy Supply/Zone must take from the correct supply and preserve queue semantics as required by that effect. An effect that searches DISCARDED Energy must not search the supply instead.
  [LOCKED] When Energy is discarded from an attached Pokémon or by a card cost, Basic Energy goes into the owner's separate Basic ENERGY DISCARD, and physical Special Energy goes into the owner's normal card discard (unless printed card text or a properly documented exception says otherwise).
  [LOCKED] A Knocked Out Pokémon's attached Basic Energy also goes into the separate Basic Energy discard. This is essential for later Energy recovery and must not vanish into the infinite pool.
  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-015: A generic deck search for ANY card (for example Base Set Computer Search) must search ONLY the actual draw deck. It must NOT generate Basic Energy from the Zone. The Zone substitution is permitted ONLY for an explicit search for Basic Energy, respecting eligible types and card-specific restrictions; it never makes an absent physical card appear.
  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-016 (v0.69.210): A Power/attack that looks at the top N cards of the physical draw deck (for example EX Crystal Guardians Charizard δ Peal of Thunder) reveals ONLY those real cards. It cannot substitute or silently sample the separate Basic Energy Zone. A builder focused on such a Power should prefer compatible, owned physical Special Energy when available and eligible to attach, *before* generic Trainer filler. Where no compatible owned physical Energy is present, keep the printed limitation and warn rather than inventing Energy.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-EVO-007 (v0.69.210): When a named evolution has multiple owned prints of a pre-evolution, favour the variant with useful attacks payable by the final attacker's Energy plan. Never silently turn optional off-type attack requirements into extra Basic Energy types when a compatible variant exists.
  [LOCKED] C-EX14-CHARIZARD-008 (v0.69.210): Double Rainbow supplies two flexible units only on evolved non-ex Pokémon and reduces outgoing attack damage by 10; Peal of Thunder respects its attach restrictions. Charizard δ Metal Burn discards actual attached Energy currently providing Metal (Basic Energy to the basic ledger; physical Special Energy to card discard). Crystal Beach can change which types Special Energy currently provides.
  [LOCKED] A card that retrieves discarded Energy searches ALL eligible discard destinations, not just the visible ordinary discard pile or just the Basic Energy counts.
  [LOCKED] Recovered Energy MUST arrive at the EXACT destination specified: (1) HAND, (2) ACTIVE/BENCH POKÉMON, (3) DRAW DECK, (4) ENERGY ZONE/SUPPLY, or (5) another named zone. These are not interchangeable operations.
  [LOCKED] If a card says 'choose' Energy, the player selects eligible cards/types/targets and count. Do not silently use the first, newest, most convenient or cheapest candidate.
  [LOCKED] If a card explicitly shuffles Energy into the draw DECK, that is a real draw-deck operation even when the Energy was originally Basic Energy generated by the Zone. It does NOT merely refill the Energy Zone.

3.2a EX Emerald's new printed Energy / search constraints (added v0.69.187)
  [LOCKED] Special Metal Energy's defensive -10 applies only when attached to a Metal-type Pokémon. It does not inflict an outgoing penalty by itself, and damage reduction occurs at the card's printed stage.
  [LOCKED] Special Darkness Energy gives its printed +10 only to attacks from Darkness-type or appropriately Dark-named Pokémon. Add before Weakness/Resistance where printed; never give the bonus to arbitrary Pokémon.
  [LOCKED] Double Rainbow Energy: attach only to Evolved non-ex Pokémon, provides TWO flexible Energy units, reduces its bearer's outgoing damage by 10 before Weakness/Resistance, and discards when no longer on an eligible Evolved Pokémon.
  [LOCKED] Multi Energy: ONE flexible unit without other Special Energy attached, otherwise ONE Colorless unit. Do not turn 'all types' into multiple attachable Energy units.
  [LOCKED] Mr. Stone's Project searches Basic CARDS (including Basic Pokémon and eligible physical Basic Energy) from deck OR discard. If Basic Energy is in the dedicated ledger, a recovery effect choosing discard must find it there; return it to HAND if that is the printed destination. Preserve which source was chosen.
  [LOCKED] Lanette's Net Search finds up to THREE Basic Pokémon of DIFFERENT types and excludes Baby Pokémon; cannot silently substitute multiple cards of the same type.
  [LOCKED] EX Emerald Rare Candy may evolve an eligible Basic directly to a Stage 2 ONLY when a real compatible Stage 1 chain exists (or Basic to compatible Stage 1); respect the normal evolution timing and the actual chosen hand card. Do not silently auto-pick the first evolution.
  [LOCKED] PokéNav searches ONLY the top three real draw-deck cards, not prizes or Energy Zone, and puts a chosen Basic/Evolution/Energy card into hand with the remaining cards retained in the draw-deck order rules.

3.2a.1 PRINTED SPECIAL DARKNESS ENERGY — DIFFERENT ERA RULES (v0.69.189)
  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-013 | Do NOT collapse all Darkness Energy printings into
    a universal damage modifier. Neo Genesis neo1-104, Expedition ecard1-158,
    and Aquapolis ecard2-142 add +10 attack damage AFTER Weakness and Resistance
    (on normal Defending Pokemon damage) and can aid non-Dark Pokemon; retain
    their separate between-turn self-damage drawback when appropriate.
    EX Ruby & Sapphire ex1-93 and EX Emerald ex9-86 add +10 BEFORE Weakness
    and Resistance, ONLY when the attacker is Darkness type or has Dark in its
    name, and lack the earlier self-damage drawback. Source: the individual
    printed Special Energy rules. Apply each modifier at the correct stage in
    both attack resolution and previews, including repeated attacks where legal.
  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-014 | Double Rainbow Energy ex9-87's -10 outgoing damage
    affects the attack damage dealt to EACH target, including Bench targets,
    and applies to Swift-style attacks that ignore Weakness or Resistance.
    The -10 is NOT negated by a generic ignore-Weakness check.
  [LOCKED] C-METADATA-001 | Energy ramp or recovery means movement or creation
    of Energy, not merely extra attack damage calculated from attached Energy.
    Do not blacklist otherwise legal Energy-search Supporters from automated
    decks on the obsolete assumption that Classic has no Basic Energy searches.
    Example: EX Deoxys Lady Outing ex8-87 can search the Classic Energy Zone,
    belongs to the search/energy archetypes, and is not opponent disruption.
  [LOCKED] C-CHOICE-001 | A card naming multiple simultaneous effects must
    perform all effects on their actual recipients. EX Emerald Combusken
    ex9-25 Lava Burn deals 20 to Active plus 10 to a CHOSEN opposing Bench;
    it remains playable without an opposing Bench (base 20). Regice ex Iceberg
    Crush chooses which attached Energy to discard on a successful heads.
    PokéNav ex9-81 takes only an eligible card from the physical top three
    and lets the player reorder the remaining two (or all three if no card
    was eligible); it does not shuffle or search the infinite Energy Zone.

3.2b GLOBAL CLASSIC ENERGY SOURCE / DESTINATION CONTRACT (v0.69.188)
  DECISION 2026-11-10: The user approved broad Energy-rules alignment and the
  conversion of *fresh Basic Energy from deck searches* to the Classic Energy
  Zone. This lets Classic use real extra-attachment / energy-ramp moves, like
  Pocket does, WITHOUT mixing the two formats' mechanics or minting discard
  recovery cards. The source words matter more than the expansion's age.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-001 | Four zones are DISTINCT: ordered fresh Basic Energy
    supply; real draw deck; real hand; and real discard (Basic ledger plus
    physical discard pile). Track exact sources and destinations for each card.

  [EXCEPTION] C-ENERGY-002 | An unrestricted 'search your deck for Basic
    Energy' effect may use eligible Basic Energy from the Classic Energy Zone,
    because the normal 30-card draw deck does not contain starting Basic
    Energy. This covers energy ramp into hand OR directly onto Pokémon as
    specified by the effect; amounts and target restrictions must stay printed.
    Search may also use actual Basic Energy cards shuffled into the draw deck.
    If both sources are legal and relevant, let the player choose, rather than
    silently consume the physical cards first.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-003 | Ordered-supply requirements are strict: the normal
    one-per-turn Energy Zone attachment takes the displayed NEXT Energy.
    An effect specifying a type and explicitly SEARCHING for it may select an
    eligible matching type from the ordered supply (it is not a normal Zone
    attachment), consuming that real queue entry. A generic unrestricted
    search presents the available types. Never conjure an unspecified type or
    silently alter the displayed preview without changing the actual queue.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-004 | Anything saying 'from your discard pile', 'recover
    discarded Energy', 'attached Energy discarded', or similar MUST consume
    eligible Energy that is genuinely discarded. Search both Basic ledger and
    real discard cards, preserving Special Energy identity. A missing discard
    source produces no attachment/recovery, even when Energy Zone has supply.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-005 | Anything saying 'from your HAND' (e.g. Base Blastoise
    Rain Dance) MUST have matching physical/converted Energy cards actually in
    hand. The engine must NEVER substitute Zone Energy merely because hand is
    empty. Abilities may attach multiple hand Energy across separate ability
    uses when printed, without overriding the NORMAL hand-attachment quota.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-006 | TWO INDEPENDENT NORMAL QUOTAS per eligible turn:
    (A) one Basic Energy placed by normal Energy Zone action, and (B) one
    Energy CARD attached from hand, Basic or Special. Attaching Basic Energy
    previously generated into hand also consumes (B). Card-effect energy
    acceleration is ADDITIONAL if printed, not an unlimited ordinary hand
    attachment and not a second normal Zone attachment.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-007 | Destination fidelity is non-negotiable. 'Shuffle
    discarded Energy into your deck' returns selected actual discarded Energy
    to the DRAW DECK, even if it originated in the Zone. Likewise 'shuffle
    Pokémon and attached cards into your deck' moves attached Basic Energy
    into the DRAW DECK as physical drawable cards. 'Return to your hand',
    'attach to Pokémon' and 'put on Energy Zone' are different destinations.
    Never divert DRAW DECK returns to the supply simply to preserve 30 cards.
    The in-battle draw deck may grow above its initial amount.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-008 | Exact 'look at the top N cards of your deck',
    topdeck manipulation and a specifically drawn physical card refer to the
    actual ordered DRAW DECK, not an arbitrary or free Zone selection. The
    generic fresh-Basic search adaptation does not grant an automatic hit in
    a top-N reveal, replace a discard search or bypass a requirement to pay
    a card from hand. Evaluate unusual hybrid cards individually.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-009 | Special Energy always remains physical: no generic
    Special Energy in the Basic supply. Printed restrictions, doubling,
    duration, discard, search and attached-card identity remain enforced.
    Do not transform recovered Special Energy into a generic Basic type.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-010 | The CPU and player use identical zone rules, source
    choices, legal attachments and limits. The CPU is never allowed to create
    discarded Energy, skip costs or search a type absent from the supply.
    Both sides must complete multi-stage source/type/target choices.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-011 | This section applies to CLASSIC ONLY. Pocket keeps
    its own Energy Zone, Moltres/Gardevoir ramp, rules and deck size. Similar
    design goals are not permission to change Pocket mechanics or import
    Classic-only Energy discards into Pocket.

  [LOCKED] C-ENERGY-012 | New cards must state, in metadata and tests, the
    origin (physical deck / Zone / discard / hand), type/count restrictions,
    choice owner, destination and whether the operation counts as a normal
    Zone attachment, a normal hand attachment or additional card-effect ramp.
    Test 0/1/multiple eligible Energy, both player/CPU, type mismatch,
    generated Basic subsequently drawn from deck, and Special Energy.

  CARD-LEVEL RECONCILIATIONS INCLUDED IN v0.69.188:
    Neo Genesis Energy Charge / Time Capsule / Feraligatr Riptide: discarded
      Basic Energy enters the real draw deck, never the supply.
    Expedition Power Charge: choose up to two eligible discarded Energy
      (including Special), shuffle into real draw deck after successful flip.
    Expedition Beating Wings / Neo Pilfer / general shuffled Pokémon:
      attached Basic Energy becomes physical draw-deck cards.
    Skyridge Energy Recycle: chosen discarded Basic to draw deck; Energy
      Plant: fresh Basic from Zone when the draw deck has no physical Basic.
    Aquapolis Town Volunteers: chosen Pokémon and ledger Basic to draw deck;
      Power Plant: discard an actual Basic from hand and choose a real Basic
      from a discard source into hand. The Done button must end cleanly.
    EX Emerald Mr. Stone's Project: distinguish searching deck (physical
      cards and eligible fresh Basic Zone) versus recovering from discard.
    EX Ruby & Sapphire / Sandstorm fresh Basic searches and acceleration:
      consume eligible real Energy Zone entries; do not invent Energy types.
    Base Set Blastoise Rain Dance: strictly from actual hand cards.
    EX Deoxys Energy Charge remains the canonical drawn-from-deck example.

  [CHECK] This patch audited shared engines and representative older/newer
    cards and added outcome tests. The complete set of all possible unusual
    historical and cross-set cards is much larger than these scenarios; an
    isolated old card should not be claimed proven merely because the shared
    tests passed. Keep auditing newly discovered exceptions as sets expand.

3.3 EX Deoxys Energy Charge — permanent regression example
  [LOCKED] Energy Charge (EX Deoxys ex8-86) flips a coin; on heads, choose up to two eligible Energy cards from discard (or fewer if unavailable) and shuffle them into the user's actual DRAW DECK.
  [LOCKED] Basic Energy eligible for recovery must be taken from the Basic Energy discard; Special Energy from the regular discard. If two are recovered, the DRAW DECK count increases by two. The Energy Zone count does not increase because of this effect.
  [LOCKED] The recovered Basic Energy is represented as a physical card in the draw deck; when drawn it becomes a real hand card and follows ordinary hand attachment/card-play rules, not a free second Energy Zone allowance.
  [LOCKED] On tails no recovery occurs; if only one eligible Energy exists recover one; if none exist don't display a fake choice or create Energy out of nothing.
  [BUG / HISTORICAL] v0.69.183 incorrectly returned Basic Energy to energySupply rather than the draw deck. v0.69.185 corrected that. NEVER reintroduce that previous expectation as a passing test.
  [LOCKED] Regression must check the actual deck size/count, discard decrements, choice identity, later drawability, hand attachment cap, tails and no-eligible cases — not merely that the card action returned success.

3.4 Other Energy card examples and tricky cases
  Camerupt EX Deoxys Energy Draw: select a legally recoverable Energy and the correct receiving Pokémon; preserve printed attachment requirements.
  Metagross EX Deoxys Super Connectivity: choose eligible discarded Psychic/Metal Energy from the applicable discard zone(s), attach to a legal target and apply any printed damage counter only when the recovery happens.
  Energy Flow: where the card returns Energy to hand, it must really appear in hand, not be lost, discarded or added to the supply.
  Hoppip/Special Condition choices: when text gives a choice of conditions, allow selecting the correct condition, not a forced default.
  Moltres and other recovery effects: allow choosing eligible Energy from the right discard zone; don't silently pick a type/target.
  Special Rainbow/Boost/Heal/Scramble Energy and earlier Darkness/Metal: preserve restrictions, effects, number of Energy units provided, duration and expiry. Rainbow should not accidentally count as multiple Energy units when it grants one flexible unit.
  Energy removed by an attack cost must really be discarded, and the AI must pay the same costs. Electabuzz accumulating 10 Energy without using a discard-requiring attack is an example of behaviour to guard against.
  Cards that inspect the 'top Energy' must consume the real displayed queue order; do not choose from the infinite conceptual pool instead.

3.4c CLASSIC MAXIMUM HAND SIZE (introduced v0.69.195)
  [LOCKED] C-HAND-001 | Classic TCG has a HARD maximum of EIGHT physical cards
    in each player's hand, including the CPU. Pocket retains its own ten-card
    cap; do not apply this Classic-only adaptation to Pocket.
  [LOCKED] C-HAND-002 | Ordinary draws and Energy/deck searches cannot exceed
    eight cards. If the hand is already full, leave undrawn cards in their
    source draw deck; never consume a card while reporting no draw.
  [LOCKED] C-HAND-003 | Multi-card forced returns, prize awards and legacy
    effects may enter the hand together. After EACH actual battle action,
    bring both sides down to eight: keep newest returned/awarded cards so
    their intended effect survives, move the oldest excess cards intact to
    the BOTTOM of their owner's draw deck, and log their identities and count.
    Do not silently delete, discard or duplicate excess cards. This is the
    documented LuckyDex hand-cap conversion for otherwise mandatory effects.
  [LOCKED] C-HAND-004 | Previously saved Classic battle states with no hand
    limit migrate to eight when the next action is processed. Test draw cap,
    attacker/defender, forced-return Unown O, CPU and energy-in-hand paths.

3.5 Prize cards, deck and searches
  [LOCKED] Prize cards are a separate hidden zone and must never randomly enter the hand or be treated as ordinary draw deck cards without an explicit card effect.
  [LOCKED] Searching the draw deck cannot retrieve a card currently set aside as a Prize. Deck exhaustion, Prize claim and 'cards drawn' are distinct events.
  [LOCKED] Pokémon-ex/other prize-changing effects and special no-prize KOs override the standard prize process in their specified cases.
  [LOCKED] Preserve legitimate actions that look at or manipulate Prize cards; adapt physical-prize mechanics carefully for the converted 30-card format. Document every conversion; do not fake an arbitrary result.
  [LOCKED] Real deck manipulations (draw, shuffle, recover, look at top N, reorder, choose specific card, send to bottom) must modify the correct ordered zone and allow required choices.
  [LOCKED] Search effects can find only eligible cards present in the zone searched. Avoid 'search succeeded' messages when the card is actually in a Prize zone or absent.
  [LOCKED] The draw deck is initially 30 minus opening/prizes/draws, but may later INCREASE after card effects shuffle recovered cards (including converted Basic Energy) into it. Never hard-cap in-battle draw-deck size at 30.
  [LOCKED] When an item moves a card from draw deck to hand, or vice versa, it must preserve identity and ownership, and deal correctly with generated Basic Energy where applicable.

3.6 Attacks, Powers, Bodies and legal action timing
  [LOCKED] Implement every printed condition and clause, including optional clauses, thresholds, self-damage, extra damage, prevention, attack locks, targeting, evolution restrictions, hand counts and status conditions.
  [LOCKED] An effect cannot be treated as plain damage merely because damage is easy to implement. 'Special effect' placeholders are not an acceptable completed result.
  [LOCKED] Weakness, resistance, retreat modifiers, effects of attached Energy and Stadiums must all apply at the correct stage of resolution.
  [LOCKED] [C-ATTACK-001, 2026-11-10] A printed next-turn attack boost must persist to the designated following turn, modify ONLY the named attack (with its printed duration), and change real damage before Weakness and Resistance. The attack selection UI must show the same boosted base-damage preview; the artwork/printed card text can stay unchanged. Both player and CPU use the same rule.
  [LOCKED] [C-ATTACK-001 examples] Jungle Scyther (base2-10/base2-26) Swords Dance makes next-turn Slash 60 base instead of 30; EX Deoxys Ninjask (ex8-13) Swords Dance makes next-turn Slash 80 base instead of 30; EX Sandstorm Vigoroth (ex2-52) Focus Energy makes next-turn Slash 90 base instead of 40. These bonuses expire if the eligible following turn passes unused.
  [LOCKED] [C-ATTACK-001 coverage] An attack's own printed effect text can be empty yet still depend on a prior effect. Never replace such an attack's runtime rule with a no-op purely because its text field is blank (e.g. ex8-13 Slash depends on Swords Dance). Verify both gameplay damage and visible preview in tests.
  [LOCKED] A printed inability to use ONE attack must not disable another available attack. Example: Scyther's Slashing Strike restriction must not prevent Bounce merely because Bounce is its only other legal attack.
  [LOCKED] When an effect suppresses effects of attacks, that does not necessarily prevent damage; and prevention of extra effects must not become damage immunity (historic Machamp bug).
  [LOCKED] Pokémon Powers, Bodies and Abilities have correct activation conditions, once-per-turn limits, Bench-only/Active-only requirements, status suppression and timing. Do not offer inactive Powers as playable actions.
  [LOCKED] Stadium actions must check player ownership/turn state, conditions and actual benefit. The AI should not spam Stadium replacements or use healing at full HP.
  [LOCKED] Status conditions are individually meaningful (Poison, Burn, Asleep, Paralyzed, Confused), including immunities, removals, conditional damage, checks and actual player choices where printed.
  [LOCKED] Coin flips must happen visibly and use the correct outcomes; moves with several flips must resolve the printed number and result semantics.
  [LOCKED] Tools, Stadiums, Supporters and ordinary Trainers must be distinguished. Playing a Supporter consumes only the Supporter allowance unless a separate effect restricts Trainers.
  [LOCKED] Opponent effects can restrict Trainer use on the FOLLOWING turn when printed; they must expire at the correct time and visibly explain the restriction. Example: Manectric ex Disconnect prevents non-Supporter Trainers for the affected next turn; it does not forbid a permitted Supporter or stay locked indefinitely.
  [LOCKED] If a card is unplayable, show the reason (turn limit, prior Supporter, opponent lock, no valid target, insufficient Energy, etc.) rather than silently swallowing taps.

3.7 Clear and accurate player choices
  [LOCKED] A decision must show what DIFFERENTLY happens with each selection. Two identical art/attack/damage choices with hidden semantic differences are unacceptable.
  [LOCKED] Example: EX Deoxys Magcargo's Knock Over should show 'Keep Stadium' versus 'Discard Stadium', rather than two identical 'Knock Over · 10 DMG' cards.
  [LOCKED] Other optional effects should spell out 'discard Energy for 90 damage' versus 'keep Energy for 50 damage', or equivalent true card-specific outcomes.
  [LOCKED] Tap actual Pokémon on the board for single-Pokémon targets: Active, Bench, friendly or enemy. Do not require a duplicate-looking popup containing three identical Pokémon pictures.
  [LOCKED] Identify board targets by unique instance/UID/physical Bench slot, not by Pokémon name alone. Two identical species are separate targets.
  [LOCKED] When choosing multiple attributes (which Pokémon, which attached Energy, how many Energy, which effect), present the stages clearly and do not silently choose an arbitrary target.
  [LOCKED] Choosing a discard/deck/hand card should show recognizable card art/names and the relevant source zone, then confirm before irreversible actions when helpful.
  [LOCKED] If an effect offers skip/cancel/optional choice, preserve it. 'Skip' must not unintentionally consume a card or apply damage.
  [LOCKED] C-EX14-POWER-009 (12 Nov 2026): Charizard δ (ex14-4) Peal of Thunder is an OPTIONAL evolution-triggered Power. Offer Use/Decline BEFORE looking at or removing the top five cards. Declining MUST leave the physical draw deck, hand and discard unchanged. If used, look at up to five actual physical deck cards, choose any number of legally attachable Energy cards for one Pokémon (including zero), then discard the remaining revealed cards. Never substitute the Classic Basic Energy Zone. The CPU may decline when no physical Energy remains; ordinary Metal Burn attack costs/discards are independent of this Power.
  [LOCKED] For switching, promotion, Bench targeting and drag-to-play, highlight valid slots on the arena. Don't replace player-selected outcomes with 'leftmost Bench' defaults.
  [LOCKED] UI should retain readable optional-effect labels, even where the attack artwork is the same for every alternative.

3.7a EX Emerald cross-set Powers, Bodies, targets (added v0.69.187)
  [LOCKED] Deoxys / Deoxys ex Form Change searches another eligible matching form from the DRAW deck and retains attached Energy, conditions, damage and identity of the board slot. It does not create cards, search prizes or mix non-ex and ex. Limit shared Form Change use per turn.
  [LOCKED] Blaziken's Blaze and Rayquaza's Dragon Dance must affect actual damage AND the visible next-attack preview, at the correct pre-Weakness stage and with correct turn expiry.
  [LOCKED] Shadow Beam / Pure Power place damage COUNTERS, not regular attack damage. Respect direct targeting and allow a player to distribute counters among eligible Pokémon when printed.
  [LOCKED] Sceptile's Green Essence checks Grass Energy attached to the team's Active; Milotic ex's Mystic Scale prevents new Technical Machines and discards existing ones WHILE active, unless suppressed by legitimate other effects.
  [LOCKED] Feebas Submerge and Swablu Feathery protect only eligible Benched Pokémon, not the Active or an unrelated team member. Altaria ex's Mist and Raichu ex's Rai-shield are printed conditional damage reduction after Weakness/Resistance.
  [LOCKED] Battle Frontier must suppress eligible Colorless, Darkness and Metal Evolved Pokémon's Powers and Bodies, not unrelated Basic or other-type Pokémon. Medicham ex Wise Aura suppresses non-ex Poké-POWERS, not all Poké-Bodies; suppression must work across older expansions.
  [LOCKED] Double Full Heal removes conditions from the current player's Active Pokémon, not the opposing Active. Oran/Lum Berry trigger at the correct between-turn point, move themselves to discard when triggered, and do not apply to arbitrary targets.
  [LOCKED] Chosen attack targets MUST use the actual Active/Bench Pokémon slots, including duplicate species; a three-counter distribution must never collapse into one anonymous menu entry.

3.8 Custom/homebrew sets in Classic
  [LOCKED] Lental Snap / Florio / Founja and other player-created cards are distinct custom sets. Do not describe their effects as official retail card rulings.
  [LOCKED] Preserve user-approved balance edits and card-specific rules rather than automatically using a broad generic template.
  Known custom decisions to preserve: Small Room limits Bench to two, Battleground permits eight (not ten), Crystalbloom applies to Basic only, Murkrow once per turn, Dream Garden tails causes Sleep, and approved Pichu/Grookey paralysis handling. Other custom specifics must be checked against their source cards/current code before rewriting.
  [CHECK] A custom Stadium that changes Bench size beyond the engine's usual physical slots needs explicit state and mobile UI tests. Current core's default is three slots, so do not assume eight can physically display correctly just because a card says eight.
  [LOCKED] Prevent cross-set card-name collisions, incorrect special tags (e.g., 'Dark' versus Water for custom Magikarp GX), inconsistent descriptions and incorrect attack preconditions.


=======================================================================
PART 3A — EX DRAGON FRONTIERS GLOBAL MECHANICS (v0.69.212)
=======================================================================

C-EX15-STATUS-001 [LOCKED] Card availability is evidence based, not catalogue based. Register all 101 English prints; enable only those with supported printed outcomes. The v0.69.212 source marks 82 FULL / battle-enabled and 19 PARTIAL. PARTIAL cards remain visible in collection and inspect, but must not enter live/automatic deck generation; no generic no-op substitution. See docs/EX_DRAGON_FRONTIERS_v0.69.212_RULES_AUDIT.md for exact card IDs and blockers. Preserve the four outstanding ex14 PARTIAL cards.

C-EX15-HOLON-002 [LOCKED] EX Dragon Frontiers reprints Holon Energy FF, GL, and WP. They must behave as the equivalent EX Delta Species print, including basic Energy activation, Pokémon-ex restrictions, relevant deck tags, and CPU attachments. Reprints count toward the correct name/ownership limits. δ Rainbow Energy supplies every colour only while on a δ Pokémon; otherwise Colourless.

C-EX15-ENERGY-003 [LOCKED] Boost Energy supplies three Colourless Energy only on legal evolved Pokémon, prevents retreat as printed, and expires at the correct turn boundary. Scramble Energy is conditional on the attached Pokémon and Prize count, and may supply three flexible Energy units only while its activation requirements hold. Crystal Beach (ex14) changes all multiple-unit Special Energy to one Colourless without altering Basic Energy. Test both active and inactive conditions and CPU planning.

C-EX15-CHOICE-004 [LOCKED] Optional Powers and “up to N” card effects must offer a real stop/decline option. Holon Mentor must pay its required hand discard before searching, enforce the eligible Basic HP threshold and selection limit, and use the real physical deck. Old Rod flips precisely the printed number of coins, then offers the permitted real discard target; do not reroll when a user makes a selection. Recovery from Energy Zone versus physical deck/discard must be explicit and preserved.

C-EX15-BENCH-005 [LOCKED] Between-turn Bench effects (e.g. Flygon ex Sand Damage) must obey target stage, protection and damage events once per correct boundary, with an observable Bench-hit visual. Attack or ability state effects, Special Conditions, attack prevention, retreat changes, Tools and cross-set δ buffs must resolve on the printed side/turn, not only update text. Target selection occurs at the printed step; if an effect says switch BEFORE damage (Feraligatr δ Drag Off), it remains PARTIAL until that sequence is tested.

C-EX15-DECK-006 [LOCKED] Deck metadata and AI must respect δ type, cross-set prerequisite Energy, typed attacks, activated Special Energy, optional Bench partners, Tools, Stage 2 evolution chains and ownership/selected set rules. The 30-card deck and separate ordered Basic Energy Zone must not be conflated. Check deck output across all nine archetypes and actual saved Energy Zone, not only static metadata.

C-EX15-RELEASE-007 [CHECK] Current live-combat subset has targeted Node state tests and supported-move execution checks, but neither Android touch QA nor all cross-set card combinations has been certified. Never claim “101/101 fully implemented” until the remaining 19 effects have real outcomes and tests. The complete per-card ledger and test caveats are in the ex15 rules audit.

=======================================================================
PART 4 — POKÉMON TCG POCKET (TCGP) — SEPARATE RULESET
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] Pocket is distinct from converted Classic. Do not spread Classic-only Energy discard, Prize or deck changes into Pocket.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.185 source Pocket battle profile: 20-card deck, 5 opening cards, 3 Bench slots, 3-point victory target, and Pocket-style Energy generation. Confirm further Pocket-specific limits from current code before any modification.
  [LOCKED] Pocket card legality is by real gameplay identity and actual owned print/art records; alternate artwork cannot evade copy limits or grant copies.
  [LOCKED] Energy uses the Pocket Energy Zone and the player's selected one-to-three types where permitted. Multi-type generation is random/seeded, with visible next Energy kept meaningful.
  [LOCKED] Pocket Confusion, condition checkup, win points, Pokémon-ex points, draw/search effects, Supporters, Trainers, Tools, Stadiums and Fossils follow the applicable Pocket implementation rather than casually inheriting tabletop behaviour.
  [LOCKED] Coins, retreat, attacks, Ability triggers, Bench damage, healing, exact target selection, weakness and Energy must execute using actual rules, not a simplified approximation.
  [LOCKED] Cards whose complex mechanics are not fully implemented are marked PARTIAL/UNSUPPORTED and excluded from legal battles. Do not silently downgrade them to generic damage and label them FULL.
  [LOCKED] The card-data pipeline must preserve canonical gameplay identity, print variants, ownership, provenance, source/errata and release eligibility. New sets require reviewed records, not unverified guesses.
  [LOCKED] Pocket deck editing needs filters, clear deck, legitimate evolution chains, cards visible on screen, a playable hand and functional manual/automatic Energy choices.
  [LOCKED] Pocket battle should be card-first, responsive and touch-friendly: tap cards for attacks/abilities, drag cards into play, show attached Energy, place cards on actual open Bench positions, animate readable CPU turns and keep the selected sleeve consistent.
  [LOCKED] Developers' test decks do not award cards, wins, currency or achievements. Unsupported mechanics must show a clear reason when excluded.
  [CHECK] Earlier notes describe various implementation gaps for currently PARTIAL Pocket cards; re-evaluate against the actual latest catalogue before claiming the current percentage of FULL cards.

=======================================================================
PART 5 — ALL DECK BUILDERS / DECK LEGALITY / METADATA
=======================================================================

5.1 Required deck-building behaviour
  [LOCKED] Separate Classic and Pocket rules, card pools, copy limits, owned cards, Energy engines and legal deck sizes.
  [LOCKED] Builder output must be legal under the selected format, not merely 30/20 arbitrary card IDs.
  [LOCKED] Provide and preserve manual deck editing, automatic build/repair, save/rename/duplicate/delete/clear deck, visible selected deck, filters and card inspection.
  [LOCKED] Set restrictions, type filters (including dual types), rarity filters, owned filters, gameplay/strategy tags and select all/none are meaningful. Do not silently drop or reset filter state.
  [LOCKED] Normal builder respects owned quantities. Test Lab deliberately ignores ownership without changing actual collection or normal saved decks.
  [LOCKED] Player and CPU deck builders must use the same legality checks and correct set/card eligibility; opponent selection must accept every supported added expansion, including Gen 3 sets.
  [LOCKED] A 'random' deck should actually vary based on its chosen seed/options, and not repeat a fixed rotation of a few prebuilt lists. Preserve reasonable archetype quality while increasing variation.
  [LOCKED] A focused build around a named Pokémon / card must genuinely revolve around that card's win condition and line. Do not bolt on unrelated multi-stage lines just because they share a type.
  [LOCKED] Dual-type decks must account for real Energy needs and synergy, not only type labels.

5.2 Evolution / Energy / strategic coherence
  [LOCKED] Evolution chains must be complete enough to function, respecting allowed copies and current type/set restrictions. No orphan Stage 2s and no nonsensical multiple partially supported lines.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-EVO-002 (11 Nov 2026): An AUTOMATICALLY CHOSEN Pokémon line must progress to its highest legal, owned, battle-ready evolution available in the selected card pool. If a Stage 1 such as Quilava is chosen as the attacker, it must also include a compatible Stage 2 (Typhlosion) when the species evolves further; do not mistake a PARTIAL/unowned/excluded Stage 2 for an unavailable species evolution and call Quilava a complete finished core. If no legal higher stage can be included, choose a different automatic core. An expressly user-focused standalone Basic in a restricted historical set remains allowed; 'Basic-only' deck filters intentionally limit evolution. Never replace the player's manually saved deck without permission.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-FILL-003 (11 Nov 2026): A Fire/Water/etc. focus does not justify adding unrelated Pokémon with Colorless-only attack costs (e.g. Grass Scyther in an EX10 Fire deck). Prefer self-contained, type-coherent evolution partners and real standalone Basics. Do not use an unaccompanied evolvable Basic as arbitrary filler.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-DUAL-004 (11 Nov 2026): Detect conditional type-changing Poké-Bodies (including Dual Armor) using their real activation condition. When an auto-builder deliberately selects such Pokémon, supply the trigger Basic Energy type, while retaining types needed for printed attack costs and respecting the two-type automatic deck plan. EX10 Magcargo: Fire+Fighting; EX10 Slowbro: Water+Psychic; Rocket's Scizor ex: Metal trigger (and Rocket's Scyther ex: Grass trigger). Never call a conditional secondary type active merely because the printed effect mentions it. Explicitly incompatible locked Energy-type filters should prevent choosing that as an auto core rather than inventing a third type.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-CPU-005 (11 Nov 2026): The opponent generator must USE the full legal Energy profile returned by the shared Classic deck builder. Never replace a purposeful Fire/Fighting, Water/Psychic or other two-type evolution/ability plan with only the requested category (such as Fire). The CPU must be capable of activating the same abilities and paying the same costs as the player.
  [LOCKED] C-DECK-MANUAL-006 (11 Nov 2026): A conditional Dual Armor activation is an auto-builder *recommendation*, not a mandatory Energy cost. A manually configured deck without the optional trigger may remain legal when all printed attack costs can be paid. Its automatic Energy recommendation should include the trigger when doing so fits without dropping required attack types.
  [LOCKED] For two-stage evolution decks, consider Rare Candy or equivalent legal accelerator when the relevant set/format supports it.
  [LOCKED] Hand attachments, Zone Energy, Special Energy, attack costs and energy acceleration must all be modelled accurately in deck construction.
  [LOCKED] Don't select Stadiums, Energy Tools, healing or retrieval cards whose conditions cannot be met by the deck. Example: a Stadium boosting an Energy type absent from the deck is not strategic synergy.
  [LOCKED] Synergy tags are causal, not decorative. A sleep-lock deck should combine actual Sleep application with payoffs, condition retention, healing/stall, stadiums and consistency. A high-HP stall deck should favor healing and retreat reduction. An Energy-ramp deck should support an attacker that benefits from extra Energy.
  [LOCKED] Consider deck roles: main attacker, backup, evolution support, search, draw, Energy acceleration, Energy recovery, healing, status control, disruption, switching/retreat, Stadium/Tool synergy, counterplay and board consistency.
  [LOCKED] Do not include bad 'filler' simply to hit the deck length. Provide candidate quality and legality diagnostics rather than quietly assembling an ineffective deck.
  [LOCKED] Avoid CPU autobuild loops that appear frozen for minutes; permit progress, cancellation or bounded work so the application stays responsive.
  [LOCKED] Keep user-selected focus cards/strategy, opponent set restrictions and existing saved deck preferences when repairing a deck.

5.3 Card metadata standards
  [LOCKED] Every card entry needs correct identity (set + number), printed name, evolution stage/line, types, HP, weakness/resistance, retreat, rarity/ownership mapping, attack cost/energy, damage, printed text and every attack/Power/Body/Trainer/Special Energy rule.
  [LOCKED] Preserve alternative print art and ownership separately from gameplay identity. Prevent ID collisions, duplication and wrong imagery.
  [LOCKED] Role/status tags must be earned by actual gameplay effects. Status immunity does not equal inflicting a status. Self-sleep does not make a Sleep-control deck. Defensive prevention does not count as direct attack damage.
  [LOCKED] Metadata used by AI/deck building must distinguish healing, protection, Sleep/Poison/Burn/Paralysis/Confusion, Energy ramp, Energy discard/recovery, search/draw, spread/snipe, retreat reduction, disruption, scaling damage and other relevant archetypes.
  [LOCKED] Multi-condition choice effects should expose all permitted conditions, not a default hardcoded one.
  [LOCKED] If a card is supported only with an explicit converted interpretation, record that conversion in its set documentation and tests.
  [LOCKED] All card effect data and metadata must remain consistent with actual engine behavior; don't tag a card as viable if its mechanic is unsupported or incorrect.

=======================================================================
PART 6 — AI OPPONENT AND BATTLE EXPERIENCE
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] CPU obeys the same rules as the player: timing, Energy Zone order, retreat, evolution timing, deck copy limits, discard costs, once-per-turn actions, targeting and Trainer restrictions.
  [LOCKED] AI should actively evaluate legal choices instead of random spam. It's acceptable to randomize among approximately equal choices, not among obviously harmful and useful actions without reason.
  [LOCKED] AI should prioritize KOs, effective targets, sensible Energy attachment, evolution, healing damaged Pokémon, retreating vulnerable Actives, status synergies and efficient Trainers.
  [LOCKED] CPU should not replace a beneficial Stadium with a harmful/irrelevant one simply because it can. Consider existing Stadium and marginal value to both sides.
  [LOCKED] CPU should not repeatedly use effects with no legal targets (e.g., Super Energy Retrieval with no recoverable Energy).
  [LOCKED] CPU must pay actual Energy discard requirements. Don't allow it to keep charged Energy while claiming to use a discard-cost attack, or permanently refuse a playable move due to a mistaken cost check.
  [LOCKED] Difficulty tiers (Beginner/Intermediate/Advanced/Expert where implemented) should reflect real decision quality, not rule-breaking or invented resources.
  [LOCKED] Opponent deck building must finish in bounded time, observe set/archetype/difficulty filters, and generate actual playable legal decks.
  [LOCKED] Show CPU actions, coin flips, Trainer use, Energy movements and damage in readable central presentation at human-visible pacing. Don't instantly complete an entire hidden turn and leave the player guessing.
  [LOCKED] The CPU should make choices for defending side effects when printed ('your opponent chooses'), rather than incorrectly allowing the acting attacker to choose.

=======================================================================
PART 7 — TEST LAB / DEVELOPER TOOLS
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] Test Lab is a separate sandbox for both Classic and Pocket. Its purpose is fast effect/combo testing without collection grinding.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab can select any existing supported card regardless of normal ownership; normal battles remain ownership-restricted.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab allows placing real cards on either side's Active/Bench, into hand/deck/discard/Prize zones as appropriate, and staging damage, Energy, status and turn context.
  [LOCKED] Inert Substitute cards/targets can be used to test effects and damage without adding new collectible cards, Powers or attacks of their own.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab must use the REAL combat engine and real card selection prompts. It is not a fake scripted animation mode.
  [LOCKED] Allow clear direct selection of Active/Bench and access to deck/hand/discard card pickers. Keep the UI usable on ONE mobile screen where possible, not a sprawling desktop panel.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab decks have their OWN saved Pocket and Classic libraries, distinct from normal saved decks and actual collection. Save, rename, delete and reopen them independently.
  [CURRENT] v0.69.185 lineage includes persistent Test Lab libraries and whitelisted CPU filter preferences (schema 2).
  [LOCKED] Test Lab card filters and CPU-opponent filters are separate and must remain visible and persistent where designed. Don't fix one by accidentally removing the other.
  [LOCKED] Test Lab matches grant no coins, packs, new card ownership, achievements, regular battle wins or progression records. Do not save fake damage/board state as a real player's official result.
  [LOCKED] Normal and Test Lab game modes should not mutate each other's deck selections, collection records, filters or battle statistics.

=======================================================================
PART 8 — CARDS / EXPANSIONS: CONTENT COMPLETENESS STANDARD
=======================================================================

  [LOCKED] Add expansions in the agreed chronological/selected order; current sequence continues EX Deoxys (ex8), EX Emerald (ex9), EX Unseen Forces (ex10), EX Delta Species (ex11), EX Legend Maker (ex12), EX Holon Phantoms (ex13), EX Crystal Guardians (ex14). Future sets follow release order unless user changes direction.
  [LOCKED] New expansion work covers ALL actual cards, not just the headline Pokémon. Include Trainers, Supporters, Stadiums, Tools, Special Energy, evolution chains and Pokémon with non-obvious effects.
  [LOCKED] Use exact set IDs and card numbers with authoritative card text and errata. Do not invent unreadable text or silently create Pokémon that were not in the released set.
  [LOCKED] Inspect interactions with older cards: Rocket, Gym, Neo, Delta, EX, Special Energy, existing custom Snap sets, status effects, Stadiums and Tools. Card references to 'Dark Pokémon', 'Rocket's', 'Trainer', 'Pokémon-ex', etc. must recognise compatible older and newer cards when the printed wording intends them to.
  [LOCKED] Expand the player builder, CPU deck selector, opponent filters, Test Lab and catalogue together. Missing an expansion from opponent selection is an incomplete release.
  [LOCKED] Every new attack/Power/Body must have an accurate description and a working effect, or an explicit gated/unsupported status. Do not publish 'fully supported' based only on cards loading or two random attacks finishing.
  [LOCKED] Move metadata and synergy tags are required in the same expansion pass, not a future afterthought.
  [LOCKED] Card search, movement, prizes, discard, choice and Energy must be validated with both success and failure/edge-case scenarios.
  [LOCKED] Work can be delivered in staged batches if the user wants, but the whole-set audit and tests must be performed before claiming completion.

Release checklist for EVERY new expansion:
  [ ] Identify exact set, date, collector numbers, card roster, rarity and identifiers.
  [ ] Load and map all cards and artwork correctly, retaining existing collection/ownership data.
  [ ] Implement every attack/Power/Body/Trainer/Stadium/Tool/Special Energy with printed-effect fidelity.
  [ ] Record LuckyDex-specific conversions/unsupported aspects explicitly.
  [ ] Verify Energy Zone, physical Basic/Special Energy, hand, discard, deck and prize interactions.
  [ ] Verify conditions, prevention, status, Weakness, HP/damage and Energy cost calculations.
  [ ] Verify target selection, optional outcomes and choices on both sides.
  [ ] Test play vs same expansion, selected earlier expansions and mixed decks.
  [ ] Player manual builder, autofill/strategy builder, rarity/set filters, CPU decks and Test Lab all recognise the set.
  [ ] AI chooses meaningful actions, pays costs, uses healing and can complete matches.
  [ ] Test normal win/lose/draw/exhaustion, saved deck reload and basic Android portrait UI.
  [ ] Record exact test counts, test failures and testing limitations.
  [ ] Update this rules file, per-set documentation and change history if any rule/exception was added.
  [ ] Bump version consistently, package changed files only, give correct Termux instructions.

=======================================================================
PART 9 — TESTING / REGRESSION / RELEASE QUALITY
=======================================================================

9.1 Mandatory test matrix
  [LOCKED] A rule bug requires an outcome-level regression: verify the card, zone, counter, target and final state. 'Effect function ran' is NOT a sufficient assertion.
  [LOCKED] Test with both sides where it matters: player and CPU use the same effects; opponent-chooses and owner-chooses are different.
  [LOCKED] Test positive, negative and boundary cases: 0/1/2 eligible cards, heads/tails, no legal target, empty deck, full Bench, two identically named Pokémon, expired lock, no Energy, mixed Basic/Special Energy, prize card unavailable and status already present.
  [LOCKED] Test normal play and Test Lab. Sandbox-only success is not proof that in-match resource flows work.
  [LOCKED] After shared-engine changes, run relevant old expansion suites and the broader historical suite where feasible. Describe stale test assertions separately from actual gameplay failures.
  [LOCKED] Never alter a failing test only to force a pass. If a rule changed with user approval, update its expectation and explain the rule change; preserve a regression for the new intended result.
  [LOCKED] Check card metadata and decks as well as battle resolution: legality, ownership limits, set/rarity filters, evolution lines, 30/20 card size, energy synergy and AI archetypes.
  [LOCKED] Check interface decisions: clear labels, one visible legal target, tapped-board selection, correct slot identity, displayed Energy, no overflow/overlaps, coin positions, damage preview and opponent action readability.
  [LOCKED] Check save migration/backward compatibility, installed app ID/signer and all version labels.
  [LOCKED] If a check cannot be performed (e.g., no Android device), say so and provide a small user-focused manual test scenario.

9.2 Permanent named regression scenarios
  R01 Energy Charge: Basic and Special Energy from separate discard zones -> chosen cards shuffle into DRAW DECK -> deck count increases -> Basic Energy can later be drawn -> normal attachment cap still applies.
  R02 Manectric ex Disconnect: opponent cannot use non-Supporter Trainers on affected next turn; may use allowed Supporter; lock expires on time; clear error text.
  R03 Magcargo Knock Over: if Stadium relevant, choices display 'Keep Stadium' / 'Discard Stadium', same printed damage, correct outcome after selection.
  R04 Salamence ex Flame Jet: selecting target happens by tapping the correct opposing board Pokémon, including when two same-name cards share the Bench.
  R05 Energy Flow: returned Basic Energy appears in the HAND as playable hand cards, no silent loss.
  R06 Moltres/Metagross/Camerupt recovery: eligible Energy chosen from correct discard zones and attached to chosen targets, preserving costs and side effects.
  R07 Trainer restrictions: playing a Supporter does not on its own block ordinary Item Trainers; legitimate effect-specific locks are visible.
  R08 Scyther: an attack-specific next-turn restriction does not disable other legal attacks.
  R09 Special Energy: Darkness/Metal/Rainbow and set-specific Energy really provide printed quantities/effects; no fake multiple-unit Rainbow payments.
  R10 Pokémon-ex KOs and prize exceptions: correct multiple-Prize awards and empty-shell/no-Prize effects.
  R11 Bench: play/target/promote/evolve from correct slot, correct choice owner and no invalid automatic leftmost target.
  R12 CPU: stadium use, energy-discard costs, heal only when useful, no effect-without-target spam.
  R13 Autobuilder: random variation, complete evolution lines, genuine strategy focus, no irrelevant Energy Stadiums, 30/20 cards and copy/ownership legality.
  R14 Test Lab: owned restriction bypass only in sandbox, independent saved decks/filters, no real rewards or stats.
  R15 Full UI: readable mobile cards, landscape Stadium proportion, battle mat size, direct targeting, clear optional outcome labels.
  R16 Card support: no 'special effect' placeholder counted as complete; each included attack/card has actually executed the relevant outcome.
  R17 [C-ENERGY-001..012] Fresh Basic deck search -> eligible ordered Energy Zone; effect-based ramp can add extra attached Basic; search shows player type choice and never invents absent Energy.
  R18 [C-ENERGY-004/007] Neo Energy Charge/Time Capsule/Riptide, Expedition Power Charge/Beating Wings, Neo Pilfer, Skyridge Energy Recycle, Aquapolis Town Volunteers always put eligible discarded/attached Basic into actual DRAW DECK.
  R19 [C-ENERGY-005/006] Base Blastoise Rain Dance requires Water Energy physically in hand. Normal hand Basic/Special uses ONE quota independently of normal Zone quota; effect-based ramp is extra.
  R20 [C-ENERGY-003/008] Normal supply order stays visible. Fresh typed search may consume first matching type; top-N physical deck checks are never converted to free searches.
  R21 [C-ENERGY-004] Aquapolis Power Plant trades an actual Basic hand Energy for selected eligible real discarded Energy, including separate Basic ledger; no fake or self-refilling Zone recovery.
  R22 [C-ENERGY-013/014] Neo/e-Card Darkness bonus after Weakness vs EX-era before Weakness only on eligible attackers; Double Rainbow -10 on Swift and Bench.
  R23 [C-CHOICE-001] EX Emerald Lava Burn base+Bench, PokéNav top-three choice and actual reorder even when zero eligible; Regice ex selected Basic/Special Energy discard.
  R24 [C-METADATA-001] Metadata for real Energy recovery vs damage scaling, Lady Outing legal autobuilder scoring, CPU/player 30-card deck legality.
  R25 [C-EX16-ENERGY-001] Power Keepers Darkness/Metal/Multi reprint damage/defense/conditional provision matches equivalent EX-era printed Energy (against other Pokémon types and multi-Energy attachments); zero extra effect from the wrong type.
  R26 [C-EX16-CHOICE-002] A printed choice of opponent Pokémon and individual attached Energy is made by the PLAYER NAMED ON THE CARD, with actual Basic/Special choice and correct discard zone. When the defending player is told to choose which hand cards to discard (Mawile Pull Away), the defender chooses each card rather than suffering a random discard.


9.3 Honest release notes
  Every patch note should state: baseline version, target version, changed files, exact bugs/decisions, tests actually run (and failures), known untested areas, required previous version, extraction target, Git/Termux commands, and one or two decisive in-app verification scenarios.
  Never extrapolate 'all cards correct' from an automated attack smoke sweep. Combinatorial card interactions require targeted examples and cross-era tests.
  Never promise that Android touch has been tested if only Node/browser tests were available.

=======================================================================
PART 10 — MANDATORY LIVING-DOCUMENT UPDATE PROTOCOL
=======================================================================

THIS PART APPLIES TO EVERY FUTURE CHAT, EXPANSION, FIX AND AGREED RULE CHANGE.

10.1 At the START of each new LuckyDex development chat
  1. Read this entire file from the latest ZIP/repository before editing anything.
  2. Identify the exact app/source version and the patch ZIP extraction convention.
  3. Read recent set-release notes and relevant existing tests.
  4. Check the current request against [LOCKED] rules and [CHECK] conflicts.
  5. Do not depend solely on conversational memory; code, tests and this document must agree.
  6. If the user reports a bug that contradicts a passing test, first investigate whether the test asserts the WRONG destination, wrong player, wrong version or a missing UI interaction.

10.2 When the user approves or specifies ANY new rule
  1. Quote/restate the rule in implementation terms: who, when, zone, limits, options, visible UI, and exceptions.
  2. Assign it a permanent rule ID (for example C-ENERGY-006, C-CHOICE-012, P-ABILITY-004, AI-STRATEGY-008, GAME-ARCADE-003).
  3. Add/change the appropriate [LOCKED] clause in THIS FILE before final delivery.
  4. Mark and resolve any superseded rule instead of leaving contradictory instructions.
  5. Add at least one regression test tied to the rule ID. Add failure and edge cases where relevant.
  6. Update any set-specific documentation and player-facing explanation made obsolete by the rule.
  7. Implement the game change and verify it against older sets and both sides of play.
  8. Bump THIS DOCUMENT version/date and include its updated copy with the patch.
  9. Describe the new/changed rule and its test in the patch release note.
 10. If implementation is intentionally deferred, label the rule [LOCKED — NOT YET IMPLEMENTED], identify the blocker, and do not claim it works.

10.3 When adding a new expansion
  1. Add a dated entry under the SET REGISTER (Part 11): set code, count, status, baseline/release version, coverage tested and any conversion exceptions.
  2. Apply the Part 8 expansion checklist to EVERY card family, Trainer, Stadium, Tool and Special Energy.
  3. Add new effect patterns to a shared rule heading when the mechanic is reusable, not hidden solely in a one-off card handler.
  4. If the expansion reveals an interaction with older cards, document and test the cross-set interaction with exact card identifiers.
  5. If the new mechanic contradicts a previously agreed LuckyDex format rule, do not silently reinterpret it. Record [CHECK], propose a faithful adaptation and get user agreement.
  6. Update per-set notes, the master test matrix and this file in the same patch, even if the rules are unchanged (record 'no global rule change').

10.4 When fixing a bug
  1. Record observed wrong behaviour, correct expected behaviour, original cause and affected versions.
  2. Determine if existing rules/tests were wrong (Energy Charge .183 is the model example).
  3. Add a regression that would have failed BEFORE the fix and passes AFTER.
  4. Update this file if a rule interpretation changed, a new exception was discovered or a previous rule was expressed ambiguously.
  5. Avoid writing 'bug is a feature' merely because the current code was designed that way.
  6. Check other cards sharing the same effect path and previous expansions using that API.

10.5 When rules and implementation disagree
  A. Preserve evidence of both claims and tag the issue [CHECK].
  B. State exactly what the running build currently does versus what was agreed.
  C. Do not silently change balance/rules or falsify tests.
  D. Resolve based on the latest user decision and printed card accuracy.
